<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Extent_of_central_government_control">Extent of central government control</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Extent of central government control">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In March 2012, the Malian government lost control over Tombouctou, Gao and Kidal Regions and the north-eastern portion of Mopti Region. On 6 April 2012, the <a href="/wiki/National_Movement_for_the_Liberation_of_Azawad" title="National Movement for the Liberation of Azawad">National Movement for the Liberation of Azawad</a> unilaterally declared their secession from Mali as <a href="/wiki/Azawad" title="Azawad">Azawad</a>, an act that neither Mali nor the international community <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_recognition" title="Diplomatic recognition">recognised</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The government later regained control over these areas.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Mali" title="Politics of Mali">Politics of Mali</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Assimi_Goita,_August_2021_(cropped).png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Assimi_Goita%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png/220px-Assimi_Goita%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="265" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Assimi_Goita%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png/330px-Assimi_Goita%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/1c/Assimi_Goita%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png 2x" data-file-width="381" data-file-height="459" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Assimi_Goita,_August_2021_(cropped).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Assimi_Goita" class="mw-redirect" title="Assimi Goita">Assimi Goita</a>, interim president of Mali since <a href="/wiki/2021_Malian_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="2021 Malian coup d&#39;état">2021 Malian coup d'état</a></div></div></div>
<p>Until the military coup of 22 March 2012,<sup id="cite_ref-telegraph.co.uk_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-telegraph.co.uk-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Mali was a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_democracy" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitutional democracy">constitutional democracy</a> governed by the Constitution of 12 January 1992, which was amended in 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> The constitution provides for a separation of powers among the executive, <a href="/wiki/Legislative" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative">legislative</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Judicial" class="mw-redirect" title="Judicial">judicial</a> branches of government.<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> The system of government can be described as "semi-presidential".<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Executive power is vested in a president, who is elected to a five-year term by <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal suffrage</a> and is limited to two terms.<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The president serves as a <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief of state">chief of state</a> and <a href="/wiki/Commander_in_chief" class="mw-redirect" title="Commander in chief">commander in chief</a> of the armed forces.<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> A prime minister appointed by the president serves as head of government and in turn appoints the Council of Ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-p14_107-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p14-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The unicameral National Assembly is Mali's sole legislative body, consisting of deputies elected to five-year terms.<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Following the 2007 elections, the <a href="/wiki/Alliance_for_Democracy_and_Progress_(Mali)" title="Alliance for Democracy and Progress (Mali)">Alliance for Democracy and Progress</a> held 113 of 160 seats in the assembly.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> The assembly holds two regular sessions each year, during which it debates and votes on legislation that has been submitted by a member or by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mali's constitution provides for an independent judiciary,<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> but the executive continues to exercise influence over the judiciary by virtue of power to appoint judges and oversee both judicial functions and law enforcement.<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Mali's highest courts are the Supreme Court, which has both judicial and administrative powers, and a separate Constitutional Court that provides judicial review of legislative acts and serves as an election arbiter.<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Various lower courts exist, though village chiefs and elders resolve most local disputes in rural areas.<sup id="cite_ref-p15_111-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p15-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite continued pressure from <a href="/wiki/ECOWAS" class="mw-redirect" title="ECOWAS">ECOWAS</a>, the initial timetable for a new election in February 2022 was pushed back and is now slated for February 2024.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Mali" title="Foreign relations of Mali">Foreign relations of Mali</a> and <a href="/wiki/France%E2%80%93Mali_relations" title="France–Mali relations">France–Mali relations</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg/220px-Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg/330px-Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg/440px-Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3668" data-file-height="2684" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rutte_and_Tour%C3%A9.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Former President of Mali <a href="/wiki/Amadou_Toumani_Tour%C3%A9" title="Amadou Toumani Touré">Amadou Toumani Touré</a> and Minister-president of the Netherlands <a href="/wiki/Mark_Rutte" title="Mark Rutte">Mark Rutte</a></div></div></div>
<p>Until 2012, Mali's foreign policy orientation had become increasingly pragmatic and pro-Western over time.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Since the institution of a democratic form of government in 2002, Mali's relations with the West in general and <a href="/wiki/Mali-United_States_relations" class="mw-redirect" title="Mali-United States relations">with the United States</a> in particular have improved significantly.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Mali has a longstanding yet ambivalent relationship with France, a <a href="/wiki/French_Sudan" title="French Sudan">former colonial ruler</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Mali was active in regional organizations such as the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a> until its suspension over the <a href="/wiki/2012_Malian_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="2012 Malian coup d&#39;état">2012 Malian coup d'état</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Working to control and resolve regional conflicts, such as in Ivory Coast, <a href="/wiki/Liberia" title="Liberia">Liberia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sierra_Leone" title="Sierra Leone">Sierra Leone</a>, is one of Mali's major foreign policy goals.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Mali feels threatened by the potential for the spillover of conflicts in neighboring states, and relations with those neighbors are often uneasy.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> General insecurity along borders in the north, including cross-border <a href="/wiki/Banditry" title="Banditry">banditry</a> and terrorism, remain troubling issues in regional relations.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In early 2019, <a href="/wiki/Al_Qaeda" class="mw-redirect" title="Al Qaeda">Al Qaeda</a> claimed responsibility for an attack on a <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> base in Mali that killed 10 peacekeepers from <a href="/wiki/Chad" title="Chad">Chad</a>. 25 people were reported to have been injured in the attack. Al Qaeda's stated reason for the attack was Chad's re-establishing diplomatic ties with Israel. The base was attacked in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Anguelhok&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Anguelhok (page does not exist)">Anguelhok</a>, a village located in an especially unstable region of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-p17_118-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p17-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Mali" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Mali">Military of Mali</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Military_of_Mali" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Mali">Mali's military forces</a> consist of an army, which includes land forces and air force,<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> as well as the paramilitary Gendarmerie and Republican Guard, all of which are under the control of Mali's Ministry of Defense and Veterans, <a href="/wiki/Civilian_control_of_the_military" title="Civilian control of the military">headed by a civilian</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Mali" title="Economy of Mali">Economy of Mali</a></div>

<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Djenne_market.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Djenne_market.jpg/220px-Djenne_market.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="135" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Djenne_market.jpg/330px-Djenne_market.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Djenne_market.jpg/440px-Djenne_market.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3132" data-file-height="1924" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Djenne_market.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A market scene in <a href="/wiki/Djenn%C3%A9" title="Djenné">Djenné</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Mali_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Mali exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kalabougou_potters_(6392346).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg/170px-Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="242" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg/255px-Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg/340px-Kalabougou_potters_%286392346%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1826" data-file-height="2603" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kalabougou_potters_(6392346).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Kalabougou" title="Kalabougou">Kalabougou</a> potters</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png/220px-Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png/330px-Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png/440px-Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png 2x" data-file-width="566" data-file-height="377" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Usine_de_coton_CMDT.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cotton processing at CMDT</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_West_African_States" title="Central Bank of West African States">Central Bank of West African States</a> handles the financial affairs of Mali and additional members of the <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" title="Economic Community of West African States">Economic Community of West African States</a>. Mali is considered one of the poorest countries in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The average worker's annual salary is approximately US$1,500.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Mali.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>GDP per capita development of Mali</div></div></div>
<p>Mali underwent economic reform, beginning in 1988 by signing agreements with the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a> and the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> During 1988 to 1996, Mali's government largely reformed public enterprises. Since the agreement, sixteen enterprises were privatized, 12 partially privatized, and 20 liquidated.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> In 2005, the Malian government conceded a railroad company to the Savage Corporation.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Two major companies, Societé de Telecommunications du Mali (<a href="/wiki/SOTELMA" class="mw-redirect" title="SOTELMA">SOTELMA</a>) and the Cotton Ginning Company (<a href="/wiki/Compagnie_malienne_pour_le_d%C3%A9veloppement_du_textile" title="Compagnie malienne pour le développement du textile">CMDT</a>), were expected to be privatized in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between 1992 and 1995, Mali implemented an economic adjustment programme that resulted in economic growth and a reduction in financial imbalances<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Vagueness" title="Wikipedia:Vagueness"><span title="This information is too vague. (July 2021)">vague</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>. The programme increased social and economic conditions<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Vagueness" title="Wikipedia:Vagueness"><span title="This information is too vague. (July 2021)">vague</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>, and led to Mali joining the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> on 31 May 1995.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mali is also a member of the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_the_Harmonization_of_Business_Law_in_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Organization for the Harmonization of Business Law in Africa">Organization for the Harmonization of Business Law in Africa</a> (<a href="/wiki/OHADA" title="OHADA">OHADA</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-ohada.com_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ohada.com-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The gross domestic product (GDP) has risen since. In 2002, the GDP amounted to US$3.4&#160;billion,<sup id="cite_ref-p9_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p9-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> and increased to US$5.8&#160;billion in 2005,<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> which amounts to an approximately 17.6% annual growth rate.
</p><p>Mali is a part of the "Franc Zone" (<i>Zone Franc</i>), which means that it uses the <a href="/wiki/CFA_franc" title="CFA franc">CFA franc</a>. Mali is connected with the French government by agreement since 1962 (creation of <a href="/wiki/BCEAO" class="mw-redirect" title="BCEAO">BCEAO</a>). Today all seven countries of BCEAO (including Mali) are connected to French Central Bank.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Mali's key industry is agriculture. Cotton is the country's largest crop export and is exported west throughout Senegal and Ivory Coast.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldenhope_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldenhope-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-marshall_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marshall-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> During 2002, 620,000 tons of cotton were produced in Mali but cotton prices declined significantly in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldenhope_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldenhope-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-marshall_128-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marshall-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In addition to cotton, Mali produces rice, <a href="/wiki/Millet" title="Millet">millet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">corn</a>, vegetables, tobacco, and tree crops. Gold, livestock and agriculture amount to 80% of Mali's exports.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eighty percent of Malian workers are employed in agriculture. 15% of Malian workers are employed in the service sector.<sup id="cite_ref-marshall_128-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marshall-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Seasonal variations lead to regular <a href="/wiki/Temporary_employment" class="mw-redirect" title="Temporary employment">temporary unemployment</a> of agricultural workers.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mining">Mining</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Mining">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1991, with the assistance of the <a href="/wiki/International_Development_Association" title="International Development Association">International Development Association</a>, Mali relaxed the enforcement of mining codes which led to renewed foreign interest and investment in the mining industry.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Gold is mined in the southern region and Mali has the third highest gold production in Africa (after South Africa and <a href="/wiki/Ghana" title="Ghana">Ghana</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Goldenhope_127-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldenhope-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The emergence of gold as Mali's leading export product since 1999 has helped mitigate some of the negative impact of the cotton and Ivory Coast crises.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Other natural resources include <a href="/wiki/Kaolin" class="mw-redirect" title="Kaolin">kaolin</a>, salt, <a href="/wiki/Phosphate" title="Phosphate">phosphate</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Limestone" title="Limestone">limestone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Energy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Mali" title="List of power stations in Mali">List of power stations in Mali</a></div>
<p>Electricity and water are maintained by the Energie du Mali, or EDM, and textiles are generated by Industry Textile du Mali, or ITEMA.<sup id="cite_ref-state_122-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Mali has made efficient use of <a href="/wiki/Hydroelectricity" title="Hydroelectricity">hydroelectricity</a>, consisting of over half of Mali's electrical power. In 2002, 700 <a href="/wiki/KWh#Multiples" class="mw-redirect" title="KWh">GWh</a> of hydroelectric power were produced in Mali.<sup id="cite_ref-marshall_128-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-marshall-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Energie du Mali is an electric company that provides electricity to Mali citizens. Only 55% of the population in cities have access to EDM.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport_infrastructure">Transport infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Transport infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Mali" title="Transport in Mali">Transport in Mali</a></div>
<p>In Mali, there is a railway that connects to bordering countries. There are also approximately 29 airports of which 8 have paved runways. Urban areas are known for their large quantity of green and white <a href="/wiki/Taxicab" class="mw-redirect" title="Taxicab">taxicabs</a>. A significant sum of the population is dependent on <a href="/wiki/Public_transportation" class="mw-redirect" title="Public transportation">public transportation</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg/170px-Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg/255px-Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg/340px-Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2336" data-file-height="3504" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_-_Bozo_girl_in_Bamako.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Bozo_people" title="Bozo people">Bozo</a> girl in Bamako</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Mali" title="Demographics of Mali">Demographics of Mali</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="float: right; margin-left: 10px">

<th colspan="2" style="text-align:center; background:#cfb;">Population in Mali<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>

<p>In 2021, Mali's population was an estimated 21.9&#160;million<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>. The population is predominantly rural (68% in 2002), and 5%–10% of Malians are <a href="/wiki/Nomad" title="Nomad">nomadic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> More than 90% of the population lives in the southern part of the country, especially in <a href="/wiki/Bamako" title="Bamako">Bamako</a>, which has over 2&#160;million residents.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2007, about 48% of Malians were younger than 12 years old, 49% were 15–64 years old, and 3% were 65 and older.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The median age was 15.9 years.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Birth_rate" title="Birth rate">birth rate</a> in 2014 is 45.53 births per 1,000, and the <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (in 2012) was 6.4 children per woman.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Death_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Death rate">death rate</a> in 2007 was 16.5 deaths per 1,000.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">Life expectancy</a> at birth was 53.06 years total (51.43 for males and 54.73 for females).<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Mali has one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_infant_mortality_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by infant mortality rate">world's highest rates</a> of <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">infant mortality</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> with 106 deaths per 1,000 live births in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Largest_cities_in_Mali">Largest cities in Mali</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Largest cities in Mali">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For a more comprehensive list, see <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Mali" title="List of cities in Mali">List of cities in Mali</a>.</div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali1974-151_hg.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Mali1974-151_hg.jpg/220px-Mali1974-151_hg.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Mali1974-151_hg.jpg/330px-Mali1974-151_hg.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Mali1974-151_hg.jpg/440px-Mali1974-151_hg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3982" data-file-height="2668" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali1974-151_hg.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Tuareg_people" title="Tuareg people">Tuareg</a> are historic, nomadic inhabitants of northern Mali.</div></div></div>
<p>Mali's population encompasses a number of <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan" class="mw-redirect" title="Sub-Saharan">sub-Saharan</a> ethnic groups.
The <a href="/wiki/Bambara_people" title="Bambara people">Bambara</a> (<a href="/wiki/Bambara_language" title="Bambara language">Bambara</a>: <i lang="bm">Bamanankaw</i>) are by far the largest single ethnic group, making up 36.5% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Collectively, the Bambara, <a href="/wiki/Soninke_people" title="Soninke people">Soninké</a>, <a href="/wiki/Khassonk%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Khassonké">Khassonké</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_people" title="Mandinka people">Malinké</a> (also called <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_language" title="Mandinka language">Mandinka</a>), all part of the broader <a href="/wiki/Mand%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Mandé">Mandé</a> group, constitute 50% of Mali's population.<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Other significant groups are the <a href="/wiki/Fula_people" title="Fula people">Fula</a> (French: <i lang="fr">Peul</i>; <a href="/wiki/Fula_language" title="Fula language">Fula</a>: <i lang="ff-Latn">Fulɓe</i>) (17%), <a href="/wiki/Gur_languages" title="Gur languages">Voltaic</a> (12%), <a href="/wiki/Songhai_people" title="Songhai people">Songhai</a> (6%), and <a href="/wiki/Tuareg_people" title="Tuareg people">Tuareg</a> and <a href="/wiki/Moors" title="Moors">Moor</a> (10%).<sup id="cite_ref-factbook_121-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factbook-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> In Mali as well as <a href="/wiki/Niger" title="Niger">Niger</a>, the Moors are also known as <a href="/wiki/Azawagh_Arabs" title="Azawagh Arabs">Azawagh Arabs</a>, named after the <a href="/wiki/Azawagh" title="Azawagh">Azawagh</a> region of the <a href="/wiki/Sahara" title="Sahara">Sahara</a>. They speak mainly <a href="/wiki/Hassaniya_Arabic" title="Hassaniya Arabic">Hassaniya Arabic</a> which is one of the regional <a href="/wiki/Varieties_of_Arabic" title="Varieties of Arabic">varieties of Arabic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the far north, there is a division between <a href="/wiki/Berber_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Berber people">Berber</a>-descended <a href="/wiki/Tuareg_people" title="Tuareg people">Tuareg</a> nomad populations and the darker-skinned Bella or <a href="/wiki/Tamasheq" class="mw-redirect" title="Tamasheq">Tamasheq</a> people, due to the historical spread of <a href="/wiki/Slavery_in_Mali" title="Slavery in Mali">slavery</a> in the region.
</p><p>An estimated 800,000 people in Mali are descended from <a href="/wiki/Slavery" title="Slavery">slaves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-slaves_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-slaves-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Slavery in Mali has persisted for centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Arabic population kept slaves well into the 20th century, until slavery was suppressed by <a href="/wiki/French_Sudan" title="French Sudan">French authorities</a> around the mid-20th century. There still persist certain hereditary servitude relationships,<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> and according to some estimates, even today approximately 200,000 Malians are still enslaved.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mixed European/African descendants of Muslims of <a href="/wiki/Spanish_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Spanish people">Spanish</a>, as well some French, Irish, Italian and Portuguese origins live in Mali, they are known as the <a href="/wiki/Arma_people" title="Arma people">Arma people</a> (1% of the nation's population).<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Mali has enjoyed a reasonably good inter-ethnic relationships based on the long history of coexistence, some hereditary servitude and bondage relationship exist, as well as ethnic tension between settled <a href="/wiki/Songhai_people" title="Songhai people">Songhai</a> and nomadic Tuaregs of the north.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Due to a backlash against the northern population after independence, Mali is now in a situation where both groups complain about discrimination on the part of the other group.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> This conflict also plays a role in the continuing <a href="/wiki/Northern_Mali_conflict_(2012%E2%80%93present)" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Mali conflict (2012–present)">Northern Mali conflict</a> where there is a tension between both Tuaregs and the Malian government, and the Tuaregs and radical <a href="/wiki/Islamist" class="mw-redirect" title="Islamist">Islamists</a> who are trying to establish <a href="/wiki/Sharia_law" class="mw-redirect" title="Sharia law">sharia law</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
