<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Mali" title="Languages of Mali">Languages of Mali</a></div>
<p>While the country's official language is <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>, the <i><a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua franca</a></i> in Mali is <a href="/wiki/Bambara_language" title="Bambara language">Bambara</a>, which about 80 percent of the population can communicate in.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Due to deteriorating relations between the Malian military junta and the French government, the Mali government announced making Bambara the official language in January 2022, although this proposal has not yet been official.<sup id="cite_ref-2022endfrench_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2022endfrench-145">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Over 40 other <a href="/wiki/African_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="African languages">African languages</a> are spoken by the various ethnic groups of Mali.<sup id="cite_ref-p6_133-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p6-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the 2009 census, the languages spoken in Mali were <a href="/wiki/Bambara_language" title="Bambara language">Bambara</a> by 51.5%, <a href="/wiki/Fula_language" title="Fula language">Fula</a> by 8.3%, <a href="/wiki/Dogon_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Dogon language">Dogon</a> by 6.6% <a href="/wiki/Sonink%C3%A9_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Soninké language">Soninké</a> by 5.7%, <a href="/wiki/Songhai_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Songhai language">Songhai</a> by 5.3%, <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_language" title="Mandinka language">Mandinka</a> by 5.2%, <a href="/wiki/Minianka_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Minianka language">Minianka</a> by 3.8%, <a href="/wiki/Tamasheq_language" title="Tamasheq language">Tamasheq</a> by 3.2%, <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A9noufo_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Sénoufo language">Sénoufo</a> by 2%, <a href="/wiki/Bobo_language" title="Bobo language">Bobo</a> by 1.9%, <a href="/wiki/Bozo_language" title="Bozo language">Tieyaxo Bozo</a> by 1.6%, <a href="/wiki/Kassonke_language" title="Kassonke language">Kassonké</a> by 1.1%, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Maure_language&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Maure language (page does not exist)">Maure</a> by 1%, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Dafing_language&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Dafing language (page does not exist)">Dafing</a> by 0.4%, <a href="/wiki/Samogo_languages" title="Samogo languages">Samogo</a> by 0.4%, <a href="/wiki/Arabic" title="Arabic">Arabic</a> (<a href="/wiki/Hassaniya_Arabic" title="Hassaniya Arabic">Hassaniya</a>) by 0.3%, other <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Mali" title="Languages of Mali">Malian languages</a> by 0.5%, other African languages by 0.2%, Foreign languages by 0.2%, and 0.7% didn't declare their language.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Mali" title="Religion in Mali">Religion in Mali</a></div>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">
<div style="border:1px solid silver; font-size:88%; padding:0.4em; width:auto; background: white;">
<table style="text-align:left; border-collapse:collapse; width:100%;">
<tbody><tr style="background:#ddd"><th style="text-align:center;" colspan="5"><a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Mali" title="Religion in Mali">Religion in Mali</a><sup id="cite_ref-IRFR_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IRFR-147">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup></th></tr>
<tr style="font-size:88%; height:4px;">
<td colspan="2" style="padding:0 4px; text-align:left;">Religion</td>
<td style="width:100px; text-align:left;"></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding:0 4px; width:1em; text-align:right;">Percent</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Mali" title="Islam in Mali">Islam</a></td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:green; width:90%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">90%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Christianity</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:blue; width:5%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">5%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Indigenous_religion" title="Indigenous religion">Indigenous</a></td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:red; width:5%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">5%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mosque_entrance_(6862566).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg/170px-Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="264" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg/255px-Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg/340px-Mosque_entrance_%286862566%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1813" data-file-height="2812" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mosque_entrance_(6862566).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An entrance to the Djinguereber mosque</div></div></div>
<p>Islam was introduced to West Africa in the 11th century and remains the predominant religion in much of the region. An estimated 90% of Malians are <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Mali" title="Islam in Mali">Muslim</a> (mostly <a href="/wiki/Sunni" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni">Sunni</a><sup id="cite_ref-pew_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pew-148">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>), approximately 5% are Christian (about two-thirds <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Mali" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism in Mali">Roman Catholic</a> and one-third <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestant</a>) and the remaining 5% adhere to <a href="/wiki/Traditional_African_religions" title="Traditional African religions">traditional African religions</a> such as the <a href="/wiki/Dogon_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Dogon religion">Dogon religion</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-IRFR_147-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IRFR-147">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Atheism" title="Atheism">Atheism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Agnosticism" title="Agnosticism">agnosticism</a> are believed to be rare among Malians, most of whom practice their religion daily.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitution establishes a <a href="/wiki/Secular_state" title="Secular state">secular state</a> and provides for <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion" title="Freedom of religion">freedom of religion</a>, and the government largely respects this right.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Islam as historically practiced in Mali has been malleable and adapted to local conditions; relations between Muslims and practitioners of minority religious faiths have generally been amicable.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
After the 2012 imposition of <a href="/wiki/Sharia" title="Sharia">sharia</a> rule in northern parts of the country, however, Mali came to be listed high (number 7) in the Christian persecution index published by <a href="/wiki/Open_Doors" title="Open Doors">Open Doors</a>, which described the persecution in the north as severe.<sup id="cite_ref-DeutscheWelle_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DeutscheWelle-150">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-OpenDoor_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OpenDoor-151">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Mali" title="Education in Mali">Education in Mali</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg/220px-Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg/330px-Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg/440px-Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg 2x" data-file-width="566" data-file-height="424" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Lyc%C3%A9ens_kati.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>High school students in <a href="/wiki/Kati" class="mw-redirect" title="Kati">Kati</a></div></div></div>
<p>Public education in Mali is in principle provided free of charge and is compulsory for nine years between the ages of seven and sixteen.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The system encompasses six years of primary education beginning at age 7, followed by six years of secondary education.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Mali's actual primary school enrollment rate is low, in large part because families are unable to cover the cost of uniforms, books, supplies, and other fees required to attend.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2017, the primary school enrollment rate was 61% (65% of males and 58% of females).<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> In the late 1990s, the secondary school enrollment rate was 15% (20% of males and 10% of females).<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The education system is plagued by a lack of schools in rural areas, as well as shortages of teachers and materials.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Estimates of literacy rates in Mali range from 27–30 to 46.4%, with literacy rates significantly lower among women than men.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Bamako" title="University of Bamako">University of Bamako</a>, which includes four constituent universities, is the largest university in the country and enrolls approximately 60,000 undergraduate and graduate students.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Mali" title="Health in Mali">Health in Mali</a></div>
<p>Mali faces numerous health challenges related to poverty, <a href="/wiki/Malnutrition" title="Malnutrition">malnutrition</a>, and inadequate <a href="/wiki/Hygiene" title="Hygiene">hygiene</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sanitation" title="Sanitation">sanitation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Mali's health and development indicators rank among the worst in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Life expectancy at birth is estimated to be 53.06 years in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> In 2000, 62–65% of the population was estimated to have access to safe drinking water and only 69% to sanitation services of some kind.<sup id="cite_ref-p7_149-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p7-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> In 2001, the general government expenditures on health totaled about US$4 per capita at an average exchange rate.<sup id="cite_ref-p8_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p8-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Efforts have been made to improve nutrition, and reduce associated health problems, by encouraging women to make nutritious versions of local recipes. For example, the <a href="/wiki/International_Crops_Research_Institute_for_the_Semi-Arid_Tropics" title="International Crops Research Institute for the Semi-Arid Tropics">International Crops Research Institute for the Semi-Arid Tropics</a> (ICRISAT) and the <a href="/wiki/Aga_Khan_Foundation" title="Aga Khan Foundation">Aga Khan Foundation</a>, trained women's groups to make <i>equinut</i>, a healthy and nutritional version of the traditional recipe <i>di-dèguè</i> (comprising peanut paste, honey and millet or rice flour). The aim was to boost nutrition and livelihoods by producing a product that women could make and sell, and which would be accepted by the local community because of its local heritage.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg/220px-Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg/330px-Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg/440px-Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3504" data-file-height="2336" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Village_Telly_in_Mali.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Village in the <a href="/wiki/Sahel" title="Sahel">Sahel</a> region</div></div></div>
<p>Medical facilities in Mali are very limited, and medicines are in short supply.<sup id="cite_ref-p8_155-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p8-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">Malaria</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Arthropod" title="Arthropod">arthropod</a>-borne diseases are prevalent in Mali, as are a number of <a href="/wiki/Infectious_disease" class="mw-redirect" title="Infectious disease">infectious diseases</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p8_155-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p8-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Mali's population also suffers from a high rate of child malnutrition and a low rate of <a href="/wiki/Immunization" title="Immunization">immunization</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p8_155-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p8-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 1.9% of the adult and children population was afflicted with HIV/AIDS that year,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (January 2019)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> among the lowest rates in <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan Africa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p8_155-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p8-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template"><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Link_rot" title="Wikipedia:Link rot"><span title="&#160;Dead link tagged January 2019">dead link</span></a></i>&#93;</span></sup> An estimated 85%–91% of Mali's girls and women have had <a href="/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation" title="Female genital mutilation">female genital mutilation</a> (2006 and 2001 data).<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gender_equality">Gender equality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Gender equality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2017, Mali ranked 157th out of 160 countries in the gender inequality index as reported by the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">United Nations Development Programme</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The Malian Constitution states that it protects women's rights, however many laws exist that discriminate against women.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Provisions in the laws limit women's decision-making power after marriage, in which the husband becomes superior to his wife.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Women are blamed for not maintaining the appearance of their husbands and are also blamed for the actions of their children if they misbehave, which encourages the cultural attitude that women are inferior to men.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> The lack of participation of women in politics is due to the idea that politics is associated with men and that women should avoid this sector.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Education is also an area in which boys dominate, since it is a better investment for the parents.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> As traditional values and practices have contributed to gender inequality in Mali, conflict and lawlessness have also influenced the growing gap in gender through gender-based violence.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> The unstable government of Mali has led to organizations like USAID attempting to improve the lives of the people, mainly women and girls' rights in order to re-engage the development of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Gender_relations">Gender relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Gender relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Religion, the patriarchal norms, and <a href="/wiki/Gender-based_violence" class="mw-redirect" title="Gender-based violence">gender-based violence</a> are major negative factors shaping the life of women in Mali.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Patriarchal norms cause major gender inequalities and lead to male domination within the household.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Girls learn household activities like chores, cooking, childcare, etc. at a young age and are expected to take the main responsibility of household chores throughout their life. This hampers women's ability to enter the formal workforce and leads to a lack of education of girls.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Gender-based violence in Mali happens both on a national and a family level. At the national level, in 2012 the conflict in the Northern part of the country increased cases of kidnappings and rapes.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> The conflict also reduced women's access to resources, economy, and opportunities.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> At the household level, Malian women face gender-based violence through domestic violence, forced marriages, and marital rape.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_160-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> The Demographic Health Survey for Mali in 2013 stated that 76% of women and 54% of men believed physical harm towards women was acceptable if the women burnt food, argued back, went out without notifying her husband, or refused sexual relations with her husband.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Area_of_opportunity">Area of opportunity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Area of opportunity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The lack of education has increased gender inequality in Mali because not many women are working outside the household are even participating in the Public Administration sector.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> After adjusting the entrance requirements and access to education, girls still have lower enrollment rates and less access to formal education.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Drop-out rates for girls are 15% higher than that of boys because they have a higher responsibility at home and most parents refuse to allow all their children to go to school, so boys tend to become educated.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, technical and vocational education has a lower numbers of girls participating and are inadequately distributed in the country because the training centers are focused in the urban cities.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Finally, higher education for girls consist of short programs because early marriages prevent most girls from pursuing a longer term education program like those in science.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Although women do not have the same access of education, in recent decades women have been entering and representing in decision-making positions in the Public Administration sector.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Out of 147 members of Parliament, 15 were women in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Recent decades show that women are slowly joining important decision-making positions which is changing the attitude and status of women in Mali, which has led to the promotion of women's right in the political sphere.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Efforts">Efforts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Efforts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Legislation at the international and national levels have been implemented over the decades to help promote women's rights in Mali.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> At the international, Mali signed the <a href="/wiki/Beijing_Platform_for_Action" class="mw-redirect" title="Beijing Platform for Action">Beijing Platform for Action</a> which suggest that women should participate in decision-making and the convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination against Women which is the foundation to women's rights promotion.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> At the national level, Mali's Constitution has the Decree No. 092-073P-CTSP that claims equality to all Malian citizens and discrimination is prohibited, which has not been followed.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The Poverty Reduction Strategy Programme (PRSP) and the Growth and Poverty Reduction Strategy Programme under the Malian Government seek to improve the well-being of the citizens, and changes to governance and gender in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The Ministry for Advancement of Women, Children and the Family was created specifically for women and children so that their basics rights and needs get met under the law.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Although there exists legislation and policy for gender equality the institutionalization of the National Gender Policy of Mali is necessary to support the importance of women's rights.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Strengthening and the support of girls' and women's access to education and training is recommended to improve gender equality in Mali.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_162-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The involvement of international organizations like USAID assist Mali financially to enhance their development through the efforts of the improvement of women's rights.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_161-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Mali" title="Culture of Mali">Culture of Mali</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Konoguel_Mosque_tower_(6439210).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg/170px-Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="265" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg/255px-Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg/340px-Konoguel_Mosque_tower_%286439210%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1807" data-file-height="2815" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Konoguel_Mosque_tower_(6439210).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/w/index.php?title=Konoguel_Mosque&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Konoguel Mosque (page does not exist)">Konoguel Mosque</a> tower</div></div></div>
<p>The varied everyday culture of Malians reflects the country's ethnic and geographic diversity.<sup id="cite_ref-p13_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p13-163">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Most Malians wear flowing, colorful robes called <a href="/wiki/Boubou_(clothing)" title="Boubou (clothing)">boubous</a> that are typical of West Africa. Malians frequently participate in traditional festivals, dances, and ceremonies.<sup id="cite_ref-p13_163-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p13-163">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Mali" title="Music of Mali">Music of Mali</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg/220px-Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg/330px-Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg/440px-Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1818" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_Dogon_Dance.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mali Dogon Dance</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Music_of_Mali" title="Music of Mali">Malian musical traditions</a> are derived from the <a href="/wiki/Griot" title="Griot">griots</a>, who are known as "Keepers of Memories".<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Malian music is diverse and has several different genres. Some famous Malian influences in music are <a href="/wiki/Kora_(instrument)" title="Kora (instrument)">kora</a> virtuoso musician <a href="/wiki/Toumani_Diabat%C3%A9" title="Toumani Diabaté">Toumani Diabaté</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Ngoni_(instrument)" title="Ngoni (instrument)">ngoni</a> with <a href="/wiki/Bassekou_Kouyate" title="Bassekou Kouyate">Bassekou Kouyate</a> the virtuoso of the electric <a href="/wiki/Ngoni_(instrument)" title="Ngoni (instrument)">jeli ngoni</a>, the late roots and blues guitarist <a href="/wiki/Ali_Farka_Tour%C3%A9" title="Ali Farka Touré">Ali Farka Touré</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Tuareg_people" title="Tuareg people">Tuareg</a> band <a href="/wiki/Tinariwen" title="Tinariwen">Tinariwen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Khaira_Arby" title="Khaira Arby">Khaira Arby</a>, and several <a href="/wiki/Afro_pop_music" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro pop music">Afro-pop</a> artists such as <a href="/wiki/Salif_Keita" title="Salif Keita">Salif Keita</a>, the duo <a href="/wiki/Amadou_et_Mariam" class="mw-redirect" title="Amadou et Mariam">Amadou et Mariam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oumou_Sangare" class="mw-redirect" title="Oumou Sangare">Oumou Sangare</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fatoumata_Diawara" title="Fatoumata Diawara">Fatoumata Diawara</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rokia_Traore" class="mw-redirect" title="Rokia Traore">Rokia Traore</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Habib_Koit%C3%A9" title="Habib Koité">Habib Koité</a>. Dance also plays a large role in Malian culture.<sup id="cite_ref-embassy_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-embassy-165">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Dance parties are common events among friends, and traditional mask dances are performed at ceremonial events.<sup id="cite_ref-embassy_165-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-embassy-165">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Though Mali's literature is less famous than its music,<sup id="cite_ref-p29_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p29-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> Mali has always been one of Africa's liveliest intellectual centers.<sup id="cite_ref-p128_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p128-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Mali's literary tradition is passed mainly by word of mouth, with <i>jalis</i> reciting or singing histories and stories known by heart.<sup id="cite_ref-p128_167-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p128-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p28_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p28-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Amadou_Hamp%C3%A2t%C3%A9_B%C3%A2" title="Amadou Hampâté Bâ">Amadou Hampâté Bâ</a>, Mali's best-known historian, spent much of his life writing these oral traditions down for the world to remember.<sup id="cite_ref-p28_168-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p28-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The best-known novel by a Malian writer is <a href="/wiki/Yambo_Ouologuem" title="Yambo Ouologuem">Yambo Ouologuem</a>'s <i>Le devoir de violence</i>, which won the 1968 <a href="/wiki/Prix_Renaudot" title="Prix Renaudot">Prix Renaudot</a> but whose legacy was marred by accusations of plagiarism.<sup id="cite_ref-p128_167-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p128-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p28_168-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p28-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> Other well-known Malian writers include Baba Traoré, Modibo Sounkalo Keita, <a href="/wiki/Massa_Makan_Diabat%C3%A9" title="Massa Makan Diabaté">Massa Makan Diabaté</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moussa_Konat%C3%A9" title="Moussa Konaté">Moussa Konaté</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Fily_Dabo_Sissoko" title="Fily Dabo Sissoko">Fily Dabo Sissoko</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p128_167-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p128-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p28_168-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p28-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_football.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Mali_football.jpg/220px-Mali_football.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Mali_football.jpg/330px-Mali_football.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Mali_football.jpg/440px-Mali_football.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1731" data-file-height="1128" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mali_football.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Malian children playing football in a <a href="/wiki/Dogon_people" title="Dogon people">Dogon</a> village</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Football_in_Mali" title="Football in Mali">most popular sport in Mali</a> is <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-p151_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p151-169">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p55_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p55-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> which became more prominent after Mali hosted the <a href="/wiki/2002_African_Cup_of_Nations" title="2002 African Cup of Nations">2002 African Cup of Nations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-p151_169-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p151-169">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p320_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p320-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Most towns and cities have regular games;<sup id="cite_ref-p320_171-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p320-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> the most popular teams nationally are <a href="/wiki/Djoliba_AC" title="Djoliba AC">Djoliba AC</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stade_Malien" title="Stade Malien">Stade Malien</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Real_Bamako" class="mw-redirect" title="Real Bamako">Real Bamako</a>, all based in the capital.<sup id="cite_ref-p55_170-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p55-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Informal games are often played by youths using a bundle of rags as a ball.<sup id="cite_ref-p55_170-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p55-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Basketball is another major sport;<sup id="cite_ref-p55_170-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p55-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Mali_women%27s_national_basketball_team" title="Mali women&#39;s national basketball team">Mali women's national basketball team</a>, led by <a href="/wiki/Hamchetou_Maiga" class="mw-redirect" title="Hamchetou Maiga">Hamchetou Maiga</a>, competed at the 2008 <a href="/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics" title="2008 Summer Olympics">Beijing Olympics</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Lutte_Traditionnelle" title="Lutte Traditionnelle">Traditional wrestling</a> (<i>la lutte</i>) is also somewhat common, though popularity has declined in recent years.<sup id="cite_ref-p320_171-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p320-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> The game <a href="/wiki/Oware" title="Oware">wari</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Mancala" title="Mancala">mancala</a> variant, is a common pastime.<sup id="cite_ref-p55_170-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p55-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mali featured a men's national team in <a href="/wiki/Beach_volleyball" title="Beach volleyball">beach volleyball</a> that competed at the <a href="/wiki/2018%E2%80%932020_CAVB_Beach_Volleyball_Continental_Cup" title="2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup">2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Malian_cuisine" title="Malian cuisine">Malian cuisine</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malian_Tea2.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Malian_Tea2.JPG/220px-Malian_Tea2.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Malian_Tea2.JPG/330px-Malian_Tea2.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Malian_Tea2.JPG/440px-Malian_Tea2.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4320" data-file-height="3240" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malian_Tea2.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Malian tea</div></div></div>
<p>Rice and <a href="/wiki/Millet" title="Millet">millet</a> are the staples of <a href="/wiki/Malian_cuisine" title="Malian cuisine">Malian cuisine</a>, which is heavily based on cereal grains.<sup id="cite_ref-p30_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p30-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p146_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p146-176">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Grains are generally prepared with sauces made from edible leaves, such as <a href="/wiki/Spinach" title="Spinach">spinach</a> or <a href="/wiki/Adansonia_digitata" title="Adansonia digitata">baobab</a>, with tomato peanut sauce, and may be accompanied by pieces of grilled meat (typically chicken, <a href="/wiki/Mutton" class="mw-redirect" title="Mutton">mutton</a>, beef, or goat).<sup id="cite_ref-p30_175-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p30-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p146_176-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p146-176">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Malian cuisine varies regionally.<sup id="cite_ref-p30_175-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p30-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-p146_176-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-p146-176">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Other popular dishes include <a href="/wiki/Fufu" title="Fufu">fufu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jollof_rice" title="Jollof rice">jollof rice</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Maafe" class="mw-redirect" title="Maafe">maafe</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Mali" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Mali">Media of Mali</a></div>
<p>In Mali, there are several newspapers such as <i><a href="/wiki/Les_%C3%89chos_(Mali)" class="mw-redirect" title="Les Échos (Mali)">Les Echos</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Essor" title="L&#39;Essor">L'Essor</a></i>, <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Info_Matin&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Info Matin (page does not exist)">Info Matin</a></i>, <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nouvel_Horizon_(Mali)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nouvel Horizon (Mali) (page does not exist)">Nouvel Horizon</a></i>, and <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Le_R%C3%A9publicain_(Mali)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Le Républicain (Mali) (page does not exist)">Le Républicain</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_R%C3%A9publicain_(Mali)" class="extiw" title="fr:Le Républicain (Mali)">fr</a>&#93;</span></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Mali" title="Telecommunications in Mali">Telecommunications in Mali</a> include 869,600 mobile phones, 45,000 televisions and 414,985 Internet users.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_people">Notable people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Notable people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Mamadou_Namory_Traor%C3%A9" title="Mamadou Namory Traoré">Mamadou Namory Traoré</a>, Mali government minister</li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Mali&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
